### L3.S13 · plot beat · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: ★ KEY DECISION 1/3 — THE PRINCIPAL'S WANT (how to answer the want: the Park, the paper, or the race). The acquirer's two wages, named. (Turns: 3 — the decision)*
> *(The library, night: the completed map in the drawer, the want in the pocket — Hartwell Park, wanted; Rackham's scattered paper, suppressed. The acquirer's two wages, named. She does the reckoning: once for the sum, once for the nerve. The question the week has been carrying: how does she answer the want?)*
*★ KEY DECISION 1/3 — The principal's want:*
- **Fortify the Park** — *answer the want at the house.* She pours the campaign's strength into Hartwell Park — the restoration pressed, the revenues defended, the house made un-takeable. The Park, defended. The paper's race, left to the solicitor's pace. *Consequence:* Augusta approves the soldier's answer; the restoration's ledgers tighten; the scattered paper's race runs slower, and the acquirer's buyers gain ground in the auction houses.
- **Buy the paper** — *answer the want at the market.* She races the acquirer for Rackham's scattered paper — the auction houses' records, the private hands, the strongboxes. The suppression, suppressed. The Park's restoration, left to its own revenues. *Consequence:* Mr. Quill's caution proves lively — the market's prices climb; the Drummond installment's hanging hurts; the Park stands defended only by the work already done.
- **Run both races** — *answer the want everywhere.* She splits the campaign — the Park's fortifying and the paper's buying, the restoration pressed and the market entered. The war on two fronts. The reckoning, doubled. *Consequence:* the week's paper costs double — candles, sealing wax, honesty; every lane feels the stretch; but the acquirer faces a campaigner on both his wages, and the bell rings for a house that answers everywhere.
> *(She does the reckoning once for the sum, once for the nerve — and the answering is chosen, and the campaign's shape is set: the Park, the paper, or the both. The map waits in the drawer. The want waits in the pocket. The fifth week holds its breath — and the answering begins.)*
*Animation: Shared.*

### L3.S19 · plot beat · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: ★ KEY DECISION 2/3 — THE AGENT'S TRAIL (how to read the elsewhere's door: watch, follow, or knock). The brass waits in the City. (Turns: 3 — the decision)*
> *(The library, night: the map in the drawer, the brass in the pocket — the City, the counting-house, the side door, the elsewhere. Henry's man is in place — discreet, daylight. The hawk's report is filed. The question the trail demands: how does she read the door?)*
*★ KEY DECISION 2/3 — The agent's trail:*
- **Watch the door** — *read it from the street.* She posts the watching — Henry's man, the hawk's eyes, the daylight's discretion. Who goes in, who comes out, when the brass is polished. The door, read from across the street. The trail, kept cool. *Consequence:* the watching is safe — the town's eyes never turn; but the door gives up only its traffic, and the elsewhere stays elsewhere, and the brass keeps its silence.
- **Follow the quiet one** — *read him to the elsewhere.* She has Henry's man follow the quiet one past the door — where he goes after, whom he meets, what the arranging arranges. The arranger, read to his source. The trail, warmed. *Consequence:* the following risks the town's eyes — daylight has witnesses; but the quiet one leads somewhere, and the somewhere has a name, and the name can be read.
- **Knock on the door** — *read it face to face.* She goes herself — the market's face, the campaigner's — and knocks on the counting-house's front, and asks for the elsewhere by its business. The door, read aloud. The trail, set alight. *Consequence:* Augusta's aloud comes early — brass read aloud before its time; the counting-house knows it is watched, and the elsewhere moves, and the trail goes cold or goes burning.
> *(She does the reckoning once for the sum, once for the nerve — and the reading is chosen, and the trail's temperature is set: the cool, the warmed, or the alight. The brass waits in the City. The door waits to be read. The fifth week holds its breath — and the reading begins.)*
*Animation: Shared.*

### L3.S27 · plot beat · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: ★ KEY DECISION 3/3 — THE SCATTERED PAPER (the paper's market: buy, let lie, or burn the trade). The acquirer wants it suppressed. (Turns: 3 — the decision)*
> *(The City's edge, evening: the counting-house's street behind her, the brass read and unread both — the reading done, the aloud held for its time. The scattered paper's market waits: the auction houses' lots, the private hands, the strongboxes. The acquirer wants it suppressed. The question the week demands: what does she do with the paper's market?)*
*★ KEY DECISION 3/3 — The scattered paper:*
- **Buy the lots** — *race him openly.* She enters the auction houses' records rooms and bids — the émigré notes, the Hartwell paper, the mortgages' paper. The suppression, suppressed. The market, raced in daylight. *Consequence:* the prices climb — the lively market loves two buyers; the Drummond installment's hanging pinches; but the paper comes home, lot by lot, and the bell's erasure is answered with ownership.
- **Let the lots lie** — *buy only the Hartwell paper.* She leaves the émigré notes to the market and buys only what bears the Hartwell name — the house's own paper, the Park's. The trade, not hers. The house, hers. *Consequence:* the purse stretches further — the Hartwell lots cost less than the whole trade; but the émigré notes go to the acquirer's buyers, and the trade's memory goes with them, and the bell keeps the rest.
- **Burn the trade** — *buy it to bury it.* She buys the scattered paper — all of it — and buries it: the notes, the ledgers, the trade, bought up and locked in the war room's boxes. Not suppressed — kept. The paper's memory, hers. *Consequence:* the war room's boxes grow heavy — the paper's memory becomes the house's burden; the purse empties fastest; but nothing is burned, nothing is buried by him, and the remembering is entire.
> *(She does the reckoning once for the sum, once for the nerve — and the market is chosen, and the paper's fate is set: the raced, the Hartwell-only, or the buried-kept. The brass shines behind her. The lots wait ahead. The fifth week holds its breath — and the buying begins.)*
*Animation: Shared.*

### L3.S39 · Gazette sting
*Purpose: Bell's verdict on the paper's week — the paper, home; arch, precise; reward-only, never mystery. (Turns: 1 — the reading)*
> *(The morning Gazette, read over chocolate — a day late, the paper's week having kept her from it. Mrs. Nance Bell, proprietor, in her own dry voice:)*
> *"Miss Hartwell has spent the week buying paper, and the trade — so long accustomed to selling the Hartwell name for pennies — finds the name is no longer for sale. London has rarely seen a lady shop with such consequence."*
> (T1 · stance) *Rose reads it twice — the amusement first (shop with such consequence! the phrasing!), then the reckoning: the buying is public now, the paper's homecoming known, and Bell has made it formidable, which is Bell's way of making it permanent. The banker's letter sits sealed in the war room's drawer through it all — weighed, discussed, unopened, the war's schedule not yet requiring it — and she files the sting under its lesson: in London, the buyer who is seen buying has arrived — and arrival is the whole of the game.* — remembered: *the lesson: the buyer, seen, has arrived.*
*Animation: Shared (sting card).*
